  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;text-align:center;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;text-align:center;margin:0pt;"><b>NOTES TO UNAUDITED CONDENSED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS</b></p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <div>
    &#160;
  </div>
  <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="d_2025-10-01_2026-03-31" name="us-gaap:BasisOfPresentationAndSignificantAccountingPoliciesTextBlock" id="c3176504" escape="true">
   <table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="width: 100%; text-indent: 0px;"><tbody><tr style="vertical-align: top;"><td style="width: 36pt;"> <p style="margin: 0pt; text-align: left; font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt;"><b>(<em style="font: inherit;">1</em>)</b></p> </td><td style="width: auto;"> <p style="margin: 0pt; text-align: left; font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt;"><b>Basis of Financial Statement Presentation</b></p> </td></tr>
</tbody></table>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The accompanying unaudited condensed balance sheet as of <em style="font: inherit;"> September 30, 2025</em>, which has been derived from audited financial statements, and the unaudited interim condensed financial statements as of and for the <em style="font: inherit;">three</em> and <em style="font: inherit;">six</em> months ended <em style="font: inherit;"> March 31, 2026</em>, have been prepared pursuant to the rules and regulations of the Securities and Exchange Commission in accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America (&#8220;U.S. GAAP&#8221;) and comprise the accounts of Hennessy Advisors, Inc. (the &#8220;Company,&#8221; &#8220;we,&#8221; &#8220;us,&#8221; or &#8220;our&#8221;). Certain information and footnote disclosures in these unaudited interim condensed financial statements, that <em style="font: inherit;"> may </em>be otherwise included in financial statements presented in our Annual Reports on Form <em style="font: inherit;">10</em>-K, have been condensed or omitted pursuant to rules and regulations of the Securities and Exchange Commission for Quarterly Reports on Form <em style="font: inherit;">10</em>-Q. In the opinion of management, the unaudited interim condensed financial statements reflect all adjustments necessary for a fair statement of the Company&#8217;s financial position at <em style="font: inherit;"> March 31, 2026</em>, the Company&#8217;s operating results for the <em style="font: inherit;">three</em> and <em style="font: inherit;">six</em> months ended <em style="font: inherit;"> March 31, 2026 </em>and <em style="font: inherit;">2025</em>, and the Company&#8217;s cash flows for the <em style="font: inherit;">six</em> months ended <em style="font: inherit;"> March 31, 2026 </em>and <em style="font: inherit;">2025</em>. These unaudited interim condensed financial statements and notes should be read in conjunction with the Company&#8217;s audited financial statements and notes thereto for fiscal year <em style="font: inherit;">2025,</em> which are included in the Company&#8217;s Annual Report on Form&#160;<em style="font: inherit;">10&#8209;K</em> for the fiscal year ended <em style="font: inherit;"> September&#160;</em><em style="font: inherit;">30,</em> <em style="font: inherit;">2025.</em></p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The preparation of financial statements requires management to make estimates and assumptions. Making estimates requires management to exercise significant judgment. Accordingly, the actual results could differ substantially from those estimates.</p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The Company&#8217;s operating activities consist primarily of providing investment advisory services to <ix:nonFraction id="c144862039" contextRef="i_2026-03-31_ProductOrServiceAxis-OpenendMutualFundsMember" unitRef="Pure" name="hnna:NumberOfFunds" scale="0" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="INF">16</ix:nonFraction> open-end mutual funds and <span style="-sec-ix-hidden:c144862040">one</span> exchange&#8209;traded fund (&#8220;ETF&#8221;) branded as the Hennessy Funds. The Company serves as the investment advisor to all classes of the Hennessy Cornerstone Growth Fund, the Hennessy Focus Fund, the Hennessy Cornerstone Mid Cap <em style="font: inherit;">30</em> Fund, the Hennessy Cornerstone Large Growth Fund, the Hennessy Cornerstone Value Fund, the Hennessy Total Return Fund, the Hennessy Equity and Income Fund, the Hennessy Balanced Fund, the Hennessy Energy Transition Fund, the Hennessy Midstream Fund, the Hennessy Gas Utility Fund, the Hennessy Japan Fund, the Hennessy Japan Small Cap Fund, the Hennessy Large Cap Financial Fund, the Hennessy Small Cap Financial Fund, and the Hennessy Technology Fund (collectively, the &#8220;Hennessy Mutual Funds&#8221;), as well as to the Hennessy Sustainable ETF. The Company also provides shareholder services to investors in the Hennessy Mutual Funds.</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The Company&#8217;s operating revenues consist of contractual investment advisory and shareholder service fees paid to it by the Hennessy Funds. The Company earns investment advisory fees from each Hennessy Fund by, among other things:</p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="width:100%;font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;"><tbody><tr><td style="width:36pt;">&#160;</td><td style="vertical-align:top;width:18pt;"> <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#9679;</p> </td><td style="vertical-align:top;"> <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">acting as portfolio manager for the fund or overseeing the sub&#8209;advisor acting as portfolio manager for the fund, which includes managing the composition of the fund&#8217;s portfolio (including the purchase, retention, and disposition of portfolio securities in accordance with the fund&#8217;s investment objectives, policies, and restrictions), seeking best execution for the fund&#8217;s portfolio, managing the use of soft dollars&#160;for the fund, and managing proxy voting for the fund;</p> </td></tr>
</tbody></table>

   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="width:100%;font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;"><tbody><tr><td style="width:36pt;">&#160;</td><td style="vertical-align:top;width:18pt;"> <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#9679;</p> </td><td style="vertical-align:top;"> <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">preparing or reviewing materials for the Funds&#8217;&#160;Board of Trustees, presenting to or leading discussions with the Funds&#8217;&#160;Board of Trustees, preparing or reviewing all meeting minutes, and arranging for training and education of the Funds&#8217;&#160;Board of Trustees.</p> </td></tr>
</tbody></table>
   <p style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; font-size: 10pt; margin: 0pt; text-align: left">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The Company earns shareholder service fees from Investor Class shares of the Hennessy Mutual Funds by, among other things, maintaining a toll-free number that the current investors in the Hennessy Funds <em style="font: inherit;"> may </em>call to ask questions about their accounts and actively participating as a liaison between investors in the Hennessy Funds and U.S. Bank Global Fund Services.</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">Investment advisory and shareholder service fee revenues are earned and calculated daily by the Hennessy Funds&#8217; accountants at U.S. Bank Global Fund Services and are subsequently reviewed by management.</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The Company recognizes revenues when its obligations related to the investment advisory and shareholder services are satisfied, and it is probable that a significant reversal of the revenue amount would <em style="font: inherit;">not</em> occur in future periods. Management judgment is required in assessing the probability of significant revenue reversal and in identification of distinct services. Investment advisory and shareholder services are performed over time because investors in the Hennessy Funds are receiving and consuming the benefits as they are provided by the Company. Fees are based on contractual percentages of net asset values of each Hennessy Fund and recognized for services provided during the period, which are distinct from services provided in other periods. Such fees are affected by changes in such net asset values, including market appreciation or depreciation, foreign exchange translation as&#160;applicable, and net inflows or outflows of shareholders in each Hennessy Fund. Assets under management represent the broad range of financial assets the Company manages for the Hennessy Funds on a discretionary basis pursuant to investment management and shareholder servicing agreements that are expected to continue for at least <em style="font: inherit;">12</em> months. In general, reported assets under management reflect the valuation methodology that corresponds to the basis used for determining revenue. The fees are computed and billed monthly, at which time they are recognized in accordance with Accounting Standards Codification <em style="font: inherit;">606</em> &#8212; Revenue from Contracts with Customers.</p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The Company&#8217;s contractual agreements for investment advisory and shareholder services prove that a contract exists with fixed and determinable fees, and the services are rendered daily. The collectability is deemed probable because the fees are received from the Hennessy Funds in the month subsequent to the month in which the services are provided.</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">&#160;</p>

  <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="d_2025-10-01_2026-03-31" name="hnna:ManagementContractsPurchasedTextblock" id="c3176505" escape="true">
   <table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="width: 100%; text-indent: 0px;"><tbody><tr style="vertical-align: top;"><td style="width: 36pt;"> <p style="margin: 0pt; text-align: left; font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt;"><b>(<em style="font: inherit;">2</em>)</b></p> </td><td style="width: auto;"> <p style="margin: 0pt; text-align: left; font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt;"><b>Management Contracts Purchased</b></p> </td></tr>
</tbody></table>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">Throughout its history, the Company has completed <ix:nonFraction id="c144862049" contextRef="i_2026-03-31" unitRef="Pure" name="hnna:NumberOfPurchasesOfAssets" scale="0" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="INF">12</ix:nonFraction>&#160;purchases of the assets related to the management of <ix:nonFraction id="c144862050" contextRef="i_2026-03-31" unitRef="Pure" name="hnna:NumberOfInvestmentFunds" scale="0" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="INF">33</ix:nonFraction>&#160;investment funds, some of which were reorganized into already existing Hennessy Funds. In accordance with Financial Accounting Standards Board (&#8220;FASB&#8221;) guidance, the Company periodically reviews the carrying value of its management contract&#160;asset to determine if any impairment has occurred. The fair value of the management contract&#160;asset was estimated as of <em style="font: inherit;"> September&#160;</em><em style="font: inherit;">30,</em> <em style="font: inherit;">2025,</em> by applying the income approach and is based on management estimates and assumptions, including third&#8209;party valuations that utilize appropriate valuation techniques. It was determined there was <ix:nonFraction id="c144862053" contextRef="d_2025-10-01_2026-03-31" unitRef="USD" name="hnna:ManagementContractsPurchasedImpairment" scale="0" format="ixt-sec:numwordsen" decimals="-3">no</ix:nonFraction> impairment as of such date. As of <em style="font: inherit;"> March 31, 2026</em>, management performed a qualitative analysis and determined it was more likely than <em style="font: inherit;">not</em> that there continued to be <ix:nonFraction id="c144862055" contextRef="d_2025-10-01_2026-03-31" unitRef="USD" name="hnna:ManagementContractsPurchasedImpairment" scale="0" format="ixt-sec:numwordsen" decimals="-3">no</ix:nonFraction> impairment.</p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">Under Accounting Standards Codification <em style="font: inherit;">350</em> &#8212; Intangibles - Goodwill and Other, intangible assets that have indefinite useful lives are <em style="font: inherit;">not</em> amortized but are tested at least annually for impairment. The Company considered various factors, such as likelihood of continued renewal, whether there are foreseeable limits on net cash flows, and whether the Company is dependent on a limited number of investors, in determining the useful life of the management contracts. Based on its analysis, the Company considers the management contract&#160;asset to be an intangible asset with an indefinite useful life and <em style="font: inherit;">no</em> impairment as of the end of the current period.&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt;">On <em style="font: inherit;"> March&#160;</em><em style="font: inherit;">14,</em> <em style="font: inherit;">2025,</em> the Company signed a definitive agreement with STF Management, LP to purchase the assets related to the management of <em style="font: inherit;">two</em> ETFs. On <em style="font: inherit;"> January&#160;</em><em style="font: inherit;">1,</em> <em style="font: inherit;">2026,</em> the agreement was terminated. As a result, for the <em style="font: inherit;">six</em> months ended <em style="font: inherit;"> March 31, 2026, </em>the Company expensed $<ix:nonFraction id="c144862065" contextRef="d_2025-10-01_2026-03-31" unitRef="USD" name="hnna:PreviouslyCapitalizedManagementContractCosts" scale="6" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="-5">0.3</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million in costs associated with the agreement that had previously been capitalized, and the Company expensed an additional $<ix:nonFraction id="c144862066" contextRef="d_2026-01-01_2026-03-31" unitRef="USD" name="hnna:PreviouslyCapitalizedManagementContractCosts" scale="6" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="-5">0.1</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million in costs associated with the agreement incurred in the current period.</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt 0pt 0pt 8pt;text-indent:36pt;">&#160;</p>
  </ix:nonNumeric>
  <div>
    &#160;
  </div>
  <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="d_2025-10-01_2026-03-31" name="hnna:InvestmentAdvisoryAgreementsTextBlock" id="c3176506" escape="true">
   <table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="width: 100%; text-indent: 0px;"><tbody><tr style="vertical-align: top;"><td style="width: 36pt;"> <p style="margin: 0pt; text-align: left; font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt;"><b>(<em style="font: inherit;">3</em>)</b></p> </td><td style="width: auto;"> <p style="margin: 0pt; text-align: left; font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt;"><b>Investment Advisory Agreements</b></p> </td></tr>
</tbody></table>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The Company has investment advisory agreements with Hennessy Funds Trust under which it provides investment advisory services to all classes of the <ix:nonFraction id="c144862068" contextRef="i_2026-03-31" unitRef="Pure" name="hnna:NumberOfOpenEndMutualFunds" scale="0" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="INF">16</ix:nonFraction>&#160;Hennessy Mutual Funds and the Hennessy Sustainable ETF.</p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The investment advisory agreements must be renewed annually (except in limited circumstances) by (i)&#160;the Funds&#8217; Board of Trustees or the vote of a majority of the outstanding shares of the applicable Hennessy Fund and (ii)&#160;the vote of a majority of the trustees of Hennessy Funds Trust who are <em style="font: inherit;">not</em> interested persons of the Hennessy Funds. If an investment advisory agreement is <em style="font: inherit;">not</em> renewed, it terminates automatically. There are <em style="font: inherit;">two</em> additional circumstances in which an investment advisory agreement terminates. First, an investment advisory agreement automatically terminates if the Company assigns them to another advisor (assignment includes &#8220;indirect assignment,&#8221; which is the direct or indirect&#160;transfer of the Company&#8217;s common stock in sufficient quantities deemed to constitute a controlling block). Second, an investment advisory agreement <em style="font: inherit;"> may </em>be terminated prior to its expiration upon <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="d_2025-01-01_2025-12-31" name="hnna:NoticePeriodForTerminationOfAgreement" id="c144862072" format="ixt-sec:durday">60</ix:nonNumeric> days&#8217; written notice by either the applicable Hennessy Fund or the Company.</p>
   <p style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; font-size: 10pt; margin: 0pt; text-align: left">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt; margin: 0pt; text-align: left; text-indent: 36pt;">As provided in each investment advisory agreement, the Company receives investment advisory fees monthly based on a percentage of the applicable fund&#8217;s average daily net asset value.</p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The Company has entered into sub-advisory agreements for the Hennessy Focus Fund, the Hennessy Equity and Income Fund, the Hennessy Japan Fund, the Hennessy Japan Small Cap Fund, and the Hennessy Sustainable ETF. Under each of these sub-advisory agreements, the sub&#8209;advisor is responsible for the investment and reinvestments of the assets of the applicable Hennessy Fund in accordance with the terms of such agreement and the applicable Hennessy Fund&#8217;s Prospectus and Statement of Additional Information. The sub&#8209;advisors are subject to the direction, supervision, and control of the Company and the Funds&#8217; Board of Trustees. The sub&#8209;advisory agreements must be renewed annually (except in limited circumstances) in the same manner as, and are subject to the same termination provisions as, the investment advisory agreements.</p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">In exchange for sub-advisory services, the Company (<em style="font: inherit;">not</em> the Hennessy Funds) pays sub-advisory fees to the sub-advisors out of its own assets. Sub&#8209;advisory fees are calculated as a percentage of the applicable fund&#8217;s average daily net asset value.</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">&#160;</p>

  <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="d_2025-10-01_2026-03-31" name="us-gaap:FairValueDisclosuresTextBlock" id="c3176507" escape="true">
   <table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="width: 100%; text-indent: 0px;"><tbody><tr style="vertical-align: top;"><td style="width: 36pt;"> <p style="margin: 0pt; text-align: left; font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt;"><b>(<em style="font: inherit;">4</em>)</b></p> </td><td style="width: auto;"> <p style="margin: 0pt; text-align: left; font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt;"><b>Fair Value Measurements</b></p> </td></tr>
</tbody></table>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The Company applies Accounting Standards Codification&#160;<em style="font: inherit;">820</em>&#160;&#8212; Fair Value Measurement for all financial assets and liabilities, which establishes a framework for measuring fair value and expands disclosures about fair value measurements. The standard defines fair value as &#8220;the price that would be received to sell an asset or paid to transfer a liability in an orderly transaction between market participants at the measurement date.&#8221; It also establishes a fair value hierarchy consisting of the following <em style="font: inherit;">three</em> levels that prioritize the inputs to the valuation techniques used to measure fair value:</p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="width: 100%; text-indent: 0px;"><tbody><tr style="vertical-align: top;"><td style="width: 54pt;">&#160;</td><td style="width: 27pt;"> <p style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif;font-size: 10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#9679;</p> </td><td style="width: auto;"> <p style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif;font-size: 10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">Level <em style="font: inherit;">1</em> &#8211;&#160;Unadjusted, quoted prices in active markets for identical assets or liabilities that an entity has the ability to access at the measurement date;</p> </td></tr>
</tbody></table>
   <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 8pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="width: 100%; text-indent: 0px;"><tbody><tr style="vertical-align: top;"><td style="width: 54pt;">&#160;</td><td style="width: 27pt;"> <p style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif;font-size: 10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#9679;</p> </td><td style="width: auto;"> <p style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif;font-size: 10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">Level <em style="font: inherit;">2</em> &#8211;&#160;Other significant observable inputs other than quoted prices included in Level&#160;<em style="font: inherit;">1</em> (including, but <em style="font: inherit;">not</em> limited to, quoted prices in active markets for similar assets or liabilities, quoted prices in markets that are <em style="font: inherit;">not</em> active for identical or similar assets or liabilities, and model&#8209;derived valuations in which all significant inputs and significant value drivers are observable in active markets); and</p> </td></tr>
</tbody></table>
   <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 8pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="width: 100%; text-indent: 0px;"><tbody><tr style="vertical-align: top;"><td style="width: 54pt;">&#160;</td><td style="width: 27pt;"> <p style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif;font-size: 10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#9679;</p> </td><td style="width: auto;"> <p style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif;font-size: 10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">Level <em style="font: inherit;">3</em> &#8211;&#160;Significant unobservable inputs (including the entity&#8217;s own assumptions about what market participants would use to price the asset or liability based on the best available information) when observable inputs are <em style="font: inherit;">not</em> available.</p> </td></tr>
</tbody></table>
   <p style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; font-size: 10pt; margin: 0pt; text-align: left">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">Based on the definitions, the following tables represent the Company&#8217;s assets categorized in the Level&#160;<em style="font: inherit;">1</em> to Level&#160;<em style="font: inherit;">3</em> hierarchies:</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">&#160;</p>
   <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="d_2025-10-01_2026-03-31" name="us-gaap:FairValueAssetsMeasuredOnRecurringAndNonrecurringBasisTableTextBlock" id="B3401B79-EC55-42DD-AA95-1DB09C8CB5B4" escape="true" continuedAt="E260CEFF-77DA-4372-8D1A-30CD9B687335">

   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The fair values of receivables, payables, and accrued liabilities approximate their book values given the short-term nature of those instruments.</p>

   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The fair value of the <em style="font: inherit;">2026</em> Notes (see Note <em style="font: inherit;">7</em>) was approximately $<ix:nonFraction id="c144862091" contextRef="i_2026-03-31" unitRef="USD" name="us-gaap:NotesPayableFairValueDisclosure" scale="6" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="-5">39.9</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million as of <em style="font: inherit;"> March 31, 2026</em>, based on the last trading price of the notes on that date (Level <em style="font: inherit;">1</em>).&#160;The Company did <em style="font: inherit;">not</em> elect to apply the fair value option to the carrying value of the <em style="font: inherit;">2026</em> Notes under Accounting Standards Codification <em style="font: inherit;">825</em> &#8212; Financial Instruments.</p>

  <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="d_2025-10-01_2026-03-31" name="us-gaap:LesseeOperatingLeasesTextBlock" id="c3176510" escape="true">
   <table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="width: 100%; text-indent: 0px;"><tbody><tr style="vertical-align: top;"><td style="width: 36pt;"> <p style="margin: 0pt; text-align: left; font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt;"><b>(<em style="font: inherit;">5</em>)</b></p> </td><td style="width: auto;"> <p style="margin: 0pt; text-align: left; font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt;"><b>Leases</b></p> </td></tr>
</tbody></table>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: left;">The Company determines if an arrangement is an operating lease at inception. Operating leases are included in operating lease right&#8209;of&#8209;use assets and current and long&#8209;term operating lease liabilities on the Company&#8217;s balance sheet.&#160;During the fiscal year ended <em style="font: inherit;"> September 30, 2024, </em>the Company renewed the lease for its office in Novato, California for an additional <span style="-sec-ix-hidden:c144862164">three</span> years. The renewed lease will expire on <em style="font: inherit;"> <span style="-sec-ix-hidden:c144862173">July 31, 2027</span>. </em>The renewal created a long&#8209;term operating lease asset recorded during the fiscal year ended <em style="font: inherit;"> September 30, 2024. </em>There were <ix:nonFraction id="c144862165" contextRef="i_2026-03-31_LeaseContractualTermAxis-OtherLongtermOperatingLeasesMember" unitRef="USD" name="us-gaap:ContractualObligation" scale="0" format="ixt-sec:numwordsen" decimals="-3">no</ix:nonFraction> other long&#8209;term operating leases as of <em style="font: inherit;"> March 31, 2026</em>.</p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt; margin: 0pt; text-align: left; text-indent: 36pt;">Upon renewal of the lease for its office in Novato, California, the Company recorded a right&#8209;of&#8209;use asset of $<ix:nonFraction id="c144862166" contextRef="i_2026-03-31_LeaseContractualTermAxis-OfficeInNovatoCaliforniaMember" unitRef="USD" name="us-gaap:OperatingLeaseRightOfUseAsset" scale="6" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="-5">1.1</ix:nonFraction>&#160;million on its balance sheet. Right&#8209;of&#8209;use assets represent the Company&#8217;s right to use an underlying asset for the lease term and operating lease liabilities represent the Company&#8217;s obligation to make lease payments arising from the lease. Operating lease right&#8209;of&#8209;use assets and liabilities are recognized at the lease commencement date based on the present value of lease payments over the lease term. In determining the present value of lease payments, the Company uses its incremental borrowing rate based on the information available at the lease commencement date. The Company&#8217;s lease terms <em style="font: inherit;"> may </em>include options to extend the lease when it is reasonably certain that it will exercise any such options. For its leases, the Company concluded that it is <em style="font: inherit;">not</em> reasonably certain that any renewal options would be exercised, and, therefore, the amounts are <em style="font: inherit;">not</em> recognized as part of operating lease right&#8209;of&#8209;use assets or operating lease liabilities. Leases with initial terms of <em style="font: inherit;">12</em>&#160;months or less, and certain office equipment leases that are deemed insignificant, are <em style="font: inherit;">not</em> recorded on the balance sheet and are expensed as incurred and included within rent expense under general and administrative expense. Lease expense related to operating leases is recognized on a straight-line basis over the expected lease terms.</p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The Company&#8217;s most significant leases are real estate leases of office facilities. The Company leases office space under non-cancelable operating leases. Its principal executive office is located in Novato, California, and it has additional offices in Austin, Texas, Dallas, Texas, Boston, Massachusetts, and Chapel Hill, North Carolina. Only the office lease in Novato, California has been capitalized because the other operating leases have terms of <em style="font: inherit;">12</em> months or less, including leases that are month&#8209;to&#8209;month in nature. The classification of the Company&#8217;s operating lease right-of-use assets and operating lease liabilities and other supplemental information related to the Company&#8217;s operating leases are as follows:</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">&#160;</p>

   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">On <em style="font: inherit;"> October 20, 2021, </em>the Company completed a public offering of <ix:nonFraction id="c144862201" contextRef="i_2021-10-20_DebtInstrumentAxis-The2026NotesMember" unitRef="Pure" name="us-gaap:DebtInstrumentInterestRateStatedPercentage" scale="-2" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="5">4.875</ix:nonFraction>% notes due <em style="font: inherit;">2026</em> in the aggregate principal amount of $<ix:nonFraction id="c144862203" contextRef="i_2021-10-20_DebtInstrumentAxis-The2026NotesMember" unitRef="USD" name="us-gaap:DebtInstrumentFaceAmount" scale="0" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="INF">40,250,000</ix:nonFraction> (the <em style="font: inherit;">&#8220;2026</em> Notes&#8221;), which included the full exercise of the underwriters&#8217; overallotment option. The initial net proceeds received were approximately $<ix:nonFraction id="c144862205" contextRef="d_2021-10-20_2021-10-20_DebtInstrumentAxis-The2026NotesMember" unitRef="USD" name="us-gaap:ProceedsFromIssuanceOfDebt" scale="0" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="INF">38,607,000</ix:nonFraction> after considering the impact of issuance costs and underwriter discounts. The <em style="font: inherit;">2026</em> Notes bear interest at <ix:nonFraction id="c144862207" contextRef="i_2021-10-20_DebtInstrumentAxis-The2026NotesMember" unitRef="Pure" name="us-gaap:DebtInstrumentInterestRateStatedPercentage" scale="-2" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="5">4.875</ix:nonFraction>% per annum, payable on the last day of each calendar quarter and at maturity, beginning <em style="font: inherit;"> December&#160;</em><em style="font: inherit;">31,</em> <em style="font: inherit;">2021.</em> The <em style="font: inherit;">2026</em> Notes mature on <em style="font: inherit;"> <span style="-sec-ix-hidden:c144862213">December 31, 2026</span>.</em></p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The <em style="font: inherit;">2026</em> Notes are direct unsecured obligations, rank equally in right of payment with any of the Company&#8217;s future unsecured unsubordinated indebtedness, senior to any of the Company&#8217;s future indebtedness that expressly provides that it is subordinate to the <em style="font: inherit;">2026</em> Notes, effectively subordinate to all of the Company&#8217;s future secured indebtedness, and structurally subordinate&#160;to all future indebtedness and other obligations of any of the Company&#8217;s future subsidiaries.</p>

Any decision to waive fees would apply only on a going forward basis.</p>

</tbody></table>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;"><span style="text-decoration: underline; "><em style="font: inherit;">2024</em>&#160;Omnibus Incentive Plan</span></p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">Effective as of <em style="font: inherit;"> February 8, 2024, </em>the Company&#160;adopted, and the Company&#8217;s shareholders approved, the <em style="font: inherit;">2024</em> Omnibus Incentive Plan (the &#8220;Omnibus Plan&#8221;). The Omnibus Plan replaced the Amended and Restated <em style="font: inherit;">2013</em> Omnibus Incentive Plan. Under the Omnibus Plan, participants <em style="font: inherit;"> may </em>be granted restricted stock units (&#8220;RSUs&#8221;), each of which represents an unfunded, unsecured right to receive a share of the Company&#8217;s common stock on the date specified in the recipient&#8217;s award. The Company issues new shares of its common stock when it is required to deliver shares to an RSU recipient. The RSUs granted under the Omnibus Plan vest over <span style="-sec-ix-hidden:c144862240">four</span> years at a rate of <ix:nonFraction id="c144862241" contextRef="d_2025-10-01_2026-03-31_AwardTypeAxis-RestrictedStockUnitsRSUMember_PlanNameAxis-TheOmnibusPlanMember_VestingAxis-ShareBasedCompensationAwardTrancheOneMember" unitRef="Pure" name="us-gaap:SharebasedCompensationArrangementBySharebasedPaymentAwardAwardVestingRightsPercentage" scale="-2" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="2">25</ix:nonFraction>% per year. The Company recognizes stock&#8209;based compensation expense on a straight&#8209;line basis over the <span style="-sec-ix-hidden:c144862242">four</span>-year vesting term of each award.</p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>

   <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt; margin: 0pt; text-align: left; text-indent: 36pt;">&#160;&#160;&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt; margin: 0pt; text-align: left; text-indent: 36pt;"><span style="text-decoration: underline; ">Dividend Reinvestment and Stock Purchase Plan</span></p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt 0pt 0pt -1pt; text-indent: 36pt;">In <em style="font: inherit;"> January 2024, </em>the Company adopted a Dividend Reinvestment and Stock Purchase Plan (the &#8220;DRSPP&#8221;), replacing the previous Dividend Reinvestment and Stock Purchase Plan that had been in place since <em style="font: inherit;">2021.</em> The DRSPP provides shareholders and new investors with a convenient and economical means of purchasing shares of the Company&#8217;s common stock and reinvesting cash dividends paid on the Company&#8217;s common stock.&#160;Under the DRSPP and its predecessor plan, the Company issued <ix:nonFraction id="c144862244" contextRef="d_2025-10-01_2026-03-31_PlanNameAxis-DividendReinvestmentAndStockPurchasePlanDrsppMember" unitRef="Share" name="us-gaap:StockIssuedDuringPeriodSharesDividendReinvestmentPlan" scale="0" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="INF">4,921</ix:nonFraction> and <ix:nonFraction id="c144862245" contextRef="d_2024-10-01_2025-03-31_PlanNameAxis-DividendReinvestmentAndStockPurchasePlanDrsppMember" unitRef="Share" name="us-gaap:StockIssuedDuringPeriodSharesDividendReinvestmentPlan" scale="0" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="INF">3,922</ix:nonFraction>&#160;shares of common stock during the <em style="font: inherit;">six</em> months ended <em style="font: inherit;"> March 31, 2026 </em>and <em style="font: inherit;">2025</em>, respectively. The maximum number of shares of common stock that <em style="font: inherit;"> may </em>be issued under the DRSPP is <ix:nonFraction id="c144862248" contextRef="i_2026-03-31_PlanNameAxis-DividendReinvestmentAndStockPurchasePlanDrsppMember" unitRef="Share" name="us-gaap:ShareBasedCompensationArrangementByShareBasedPaymentAwardNumberOfSharesAuthorized" scale="0" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="INF">1,530,000</ix:nonFraction>, of which <ix:nonFraction id="c144862249" contextRef="i_2026-03-31_PlanNameAxis-DividendReinvestmentAndStockPurchasePlanDrsppMember" unitRef="Share" name="us-gaap:CommonStockCapitalSharesReservedForFutureIssuance" scale="0" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="INF">1,508,507</ix:nonFraction> remained available for issuance as of <em style="font: inherit;"> March 31, 2026</em>.</p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt;"><span style="text-decoration: underline; ">Stock Buyback Program</span></p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">In <em style="font: inherit;"> August&#160;</em><em style="font: inherit;">2010,</em> the Company&#8217;s Board of Directors adopted a stock buyback program pursuant to which the Company was authorized to repurchase up to <ix:nonFraction id="c144862251" contextRef="i_2010-08-31_ShareRepurchaseProgramAxis-StockBuybackProgramMember" unitRef="Share" name="srt:StockRepurchaseProgramNumberOfSharesAuthorizedToBeRepurchased" scale="0" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="INF">1,500,000</ix:nonFraction>&#160;shares of its common stock in the open market, in privately negotiated transactions, or otherwise. The program has <em style="font: inherit;">no</em> expiration date. In <em style="font: inherit;"> August&#160;</em><em style="font: inherit;">2022,</em> the Board of Directors increased the number of shares that <em style="font: inherit;"> may </em>be repurchased under the program to <ix:nonFraction id="c144862254" contextRef="i_2022-08-31_ShareRepurchaseProgramAxis-StockBuybackProgramMember" unitRef="Share" name="srt:StockRepurchaseProgramNumberOfSharesAuthorizedToBeRepurchased" scale="0" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="INF">2,000,000</ix:nonFraction>&#160;shares. As a result,&#160;<ix:nonFraction id="c144862255" contextRef="i_2022-08-31_ShareRepurchaseProgramAxis-StockBuybackProgramMember" unitRef="Share" name="hnna:ShareRepurchaseProgramRemainingSharesAuthorizedNumberOfShares" scale="0" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="INF">1,096,368</ix:nonFraction>&#160;shares remain&#160;available for repurchase under the stock buyback program. The Company did <span style="-sec-ix-hidden:c144862256">not</span> repurchase any shares of its common stock pursuant to the stock buyback program during the <em style="font: inherit;">six</em> months ended <em style="font: inherit;"> March 31, 2026</em>.</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">&#160;</p>

   <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt 0pt 0pt 8pt; text-indent: 27pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt;">For the <em style="font: inherit;">three</em> and <em style="font: inherit;">six</em> months ended <em style="font: inherit;"> March 31, 2026 </em>and <em style="font: inherit;">2025</em>, the Company included all common stock equivalents in the diluted earnings per share calculations as all common stock equivalents were dilutive.&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The Company paid a quarterly cash dividend of $<ix:nonFraction id="c144862280" contextRef="d_2026-03-04_2026-03-04_DividendsAxis-O2026Q2DividendsMember" unitRef="USDPerShare" name="us-gaap:CommonStockDividendsPerShareCashPaid" scale="0" format="ixt:num-dot-decimal" decimals="INF">0.15</ix:nonFraction> per share on <em style="font: inherit;"> March 4, 2026, </em>to shareholders of record as of <em style="font: inherit;"> <span style="-sec-ix-hidden:c144862281">February 18, 2026</span>.</em></p>
  </ix:nonNumeric>
  <div>
    &#160;
  </div>
  <ix:nonNumeric contextRef="d_2025-10-01_2026-03-31" name="us-gaap:NewAccountingPronouncementsAndChangesInAccountingPrinciplesTextBlock" id="c3176524" escape="true">
   <table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="width: 100%; text-indent: 0px;"><tbody><tr style="vertical-align: top;"><td style="width: 36pt;"> <p style="margin: 0pt; text-align: left; font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt;"><b>(<em style="font: inherit;">12</em>)</b></p> </td><td style="width: auto;"> <p style="margin: 0pt; text-align: left; font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt;"><b>Recently Issued and Adopted Accounting Standards</b></p> </td></tr>
</tbody></table>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt;">The Company has reviewed accounting pronouncements issued between the filing date of its most recent Form <em style="font: inherit;">10</em>-K, which was <em style="font: inherit;"> December 3, 2025, </em>and the filing date of this Form <em style="font: inherit;">10</em>-Q and has determined that <em style="font: inherit;">no</em> accounting pronouncements issued would have a material impact on the Company&#8217;s financial position, results of operations, or disclosures, except as disclosed below.</p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">In <em style="font: inherit;"> November 2023, </em>the FASB issued Accounting Standards Update&#160;(&#8220;ASU&#8221;) <em style="font: inherit;">2023</em>-<em style="font: inherit;">07,</em> &#8220;Segment Reporting (Topic <em style="font: inherit;">280</em>): Improvements to Reportable Segment Disclosures,&#8221; which&#160;expands disclosures about a public entity&#8217;s reportable segments and requires more enhanced information about a reportable segment&#8217;s expenses, interim segment profit or loss, and how a public entity&#8217;s chief operating decision maker uses reported segment profit or loss information in assessing segment performance and allocating resources. The Company adopted this standard in its <em style="font: inherit;">10</em>-K filing for&#160;fiscal year <em style="font: inherit;">2025.</em></p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">As of <em style="font: inherit;"> March&#160;</em><em style="font: inherit;">31,</em> <em style="font: inherit;">2026</em>, the Company operates in <em style="font: inherit;">one</em> business segment, which is its investment advisory business. The determination of a single business segment is consistent with the financial information regularly provided to the Company&#8217;s chief operating decision maker (&#8220;CODM&#8221;), and included consideration of factors such as revenue coming from a single source (investment advisory fees generated by the Hennessy Funds) and the Company utilizing the same technology, marketing, sales, and distribution programs to support all of the Hennessy Funds. The key measure of segment profitability that the CODM uses to allocate resources and assess performance is consolidated net income, as reported on the statement of income. The CODM does <em style="font: inherit;">not</em> regularly review disaggregated expense categories beyond those presented in the accompanying statements of income. The measure of segment assets is reported in the accompanying balance sheets as &#8220;Total assets.&#8221; Expenditures for long-lived assets are reported as &#8220;Purchases of property and equipment&#8221; on the statements of cash flows.</p>
   <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
   <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt;">In <em style="font: inherit;"> December 2023, </em>the FASB issued ASU <em style="font: inherit;">2023</em>-<em style="font: inherit;">09,</em> &#8220;Income Taxes (Topic <em style="font: inherit;">740</em>): Improvements to Income Tax Disclosures,&#8221; which requires more detailed income tax disclosures. The guidance requires entities to disclose disaggregated information about their effective tax rate reconciliation as well as expanded information on income taxes paid by jurisdiction. The guidance is effective for financial statements issued for annual periods beginning after <em style="font: inherit;"> December 15, 2024, </em>with early adoption permitted. The Company is required to adopt this standard prospectively in fiscal year <em style="font: inherit;">2026.</em> The Company is currently in the process of evaluating the impact of adoption on its financial statements.</p>
   <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt;">&#160;</p>

   <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt;">In <em style="font: inherit;"> November 2024, </em>the FASB issued ASU <em style="font: inherit;">2024</em>-<em style="font: inherit;">03,</em> &#8220;Income Statement - Reporting Comprehensive Income - Expense Disaggregation Disclosures (Subtopic <em style="font: inherit;">220</em>-<em style="font: inherit;">40</em>): Disaggregation of Income Statement Expenses,&#8221; as amended by ASU <em style="font: inherit;">2025</em>-<em style="font: inherit;">01,</em> which requires public business entities to disclose additional information about specific expense categories in the notes to financial statements at interim and annual reporting periods. In particular, this amendment requires public business entities to disclose detailed information about specific costs that are presented in commonly referred expense captions, such as general and administrative expenses. The guidance is effective for financial statements issued for annual reporting periods beginning after <em style="font: inherit;"> December 15, 2026, </em>and interim reporting periods within annual reporting periods beginning after <em style="font: inherit;"> December 15, 2027. </em>Early adoption is permitted. The Company is currently in the process of evaluating the impact of the adoption on its financial statements.</p>

  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt 0pt 0pt 45pt;text-indent:-45pt;"><b><span style="text-decoration: underline; "><a id="mda" title="mda" href="#"></a>Item 2.</span> &#160; &#160; &#160; &#160; &#160; &#160;</b><b><span style="text-decoration: underline; ">Management</span></b><span style="text-decoration: underline; ">&#8217;</span><b><span style="text-decoration: underline; ">s Discussion and Analysis of Financial Condition and Results of Operations</span></b></p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;"><b>Forward</b>&#8209;<b>Looking Statements </b></p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">This report contains &#8220;forward-looking statements&#8221; within the meaning of the securities laws, for which we claim the protection of the safe harbor for forward-looking statements contained in the Private Securities Litigation Reform Act of 1995. In some cases, forward&#8209;looking statements can be identified by terminology such as &#8220;expect,&#8221; &#8220;anticipate,&#8221; &#8220;intend,&#8221; &#8220;may,&#8221; &#8220;plan,&#8221; &#8220;will,&#8221; &#8220;should,&#8221; &#8220;could,&#8221; &#8220;would,&#8221; &#8220;assume,&#8221; &#8220;believe,&#8221; &#8220;estimate,&#8221; &#8220;predict,&#8221; &#8220;potential,&#8221; &#8220;project,&#8221; &#8220;continue,&#8221; &#8220;seek,&#8221; and similar expressions, as well as statements in the future tense. We have based these forward-looking statements on our current expectations and projections about future events, based on information currently available to us. Forward&#8209;looking statements should not be read as a guarantee of future performance or results and will not necessarily be accurate indications of the times at which, or means by which, such performance or results will be achieved.</p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt;">Forward-looking statements are subject to risks, uncertainties, and assumptions, including those described in our Annual Report on Form&#160;10&#8209;K for the fiscal year ended September 30, 2025, including under the section entitled &#8220;Risk Factors&#8221; in such report. Management does not assume responsibility for the accuracy or completeness of these forward-looking statements.   <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">Our business activities are affected by many factors, including, without limitation, redemptions by investors in the Hennessy Funds, taxes, general economic and business conditions, interest rate movements, inflation, the personal savings rate, competitive conditions, industry regulation, and fluctuations in the stock market, many of which are beyond the control of our management. Further, the business and regulatory environments in which we operate remain complex, uncertain, and subject to change. We expect that regulatory requirements and developments will cause us to incur additional administrative and compliance costs. Notwithstanding the variability in our economic and regulatory environments, we remain focused on the investment performance of the Hennessy Funds and on providing high&#8209;quality customer service to investors.</p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt;">Our business strategy centers on (i)&#160;the identification, completion, and integration of future acquisitions and (ii)&#160;organic growth, through both the retention of the fund assets we currently manage and the generation of inflows into the funds we manage. The success of our business strategy may be influenced by the factors discussed in the section titled &#8220;Risk Factors&#8221; in our Annual Report on Form&#160;10-K for the fiscal year ended September 30, 2025. All statements regarding our business strategy, as well as statements regarding market trends and risks and assumptions about changes in the marketplace, are forward-looking by their nature.</p>

  <p style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; font-size: 10pt; margin: 0pt; text-align: left">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;"><b>Overview</b></p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">Our primary business activity is providing investment advisory services to a family of 16&#160;open-end mutual funds and one ETF branded as the Hennessy Funds. We manage 12 of the 17 Hennessy Funds internally. For the remaining five funds, we have delegated the day&#8208;to&#8209;day portfolio management responsibilities to sub&#8209;advisors, subject to our oversight. We oversee the selection and continued employment of each sub&#8209;advisor, review each fund&#8217;s investment performance, and monitor each sub&#8209;advisor&#8217;s adherence to each applicable fund&#8217;s investment objectives, policies, and restrictions. In addition, we conduct ongoing reviews of the compliance programs of sub&#8209;advisors and make onsite visits to sub&#8209;advisors, as feasible. Our secondary business activity is providing shareholder services to investors in the Hennessy Mutual Funds.</p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">We derive our operating revenues from investment advisory fees paid to us by the Hennessy Funds and shareholder service fees paid to us by the Hennessy Mutual Funds. These fees are calculated as a percentage of the average daily net assets of each Hennessy Fund. The percentage amount of the investment advisory fees varies by fund. The percentage amount of the shareholder service fees is consistent across all Hennessy Mutual Funds, but shareholder service fees are charged on Investor Class shares only. The dollar amount of the fees we receive fluctuates with changes in the average net asset value of each Hennessy Fund, which is affected by each fund&#8217;s investment performance, purchases and redemptions of shares, general market conditions, and the success of our marketing, sales, and public relations efforts.</p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt;">On a total return basis, the Dow Jones Industrial Average was up 0.71% for the six months ended March 31, 2026. During the most recent quarter, equity prices declined as geopolitical fears coupled with prospective interest rate cuts&#160;have seemed to&#160;cause&#160;investors to reconsider the case for continued economic growth.&#160;A joint United States and Israeli military campaign against Iran has had the effect of both spiking oil prices and closing the Strait of Hormuz, a key oil and gas shipping channel.&#160;The uncertainty around the potential length of the action and its implications for oil and gas prices has caused global unease.&#160;The immediate effect of these dynamics has been rising gasoline prices, the prospect of dampened consumer spending, and the possibility of increased inflation.&#160;While the bond market had been expecting two interest rate cuts by the Federal Reserve in 2026, currently the bond market is not pricing in any rate cuts this year on account of an expectation of elevated rates of inflation returning, according to Bloomberg.</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt 0pt 0pt 8pt;text-indent:36pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt;">Long-term U.S. bond yields rose during the three months ended March 31, 2026. Higher oil and gas prices may portend a higher level of inflation, especially if a resolution with Iran is not reached soon.&#160;Before the conflict in the Middle East, inflation remained above the Federal Reserve&#8217;s 2% goal but was slowly making progress toward that goal. Projections compiled by Bloomberg estimate that the Consumer Price Index will advance by 3.1% in 2026, after increasing by 2.7% in 2025.</p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt;">The Japanese equity market was up 4.69% in U.S. dollar terms over the six months ended March 31, 2026, as measured by the Tokyo Stock Price Index. During the period, Japanese equities traded higher as aggressive corporate governance reforms, a weak yen boosting exporter profits, a return to moderate inflation, and renewed foreign investment drove an increase in share values.</p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt;">For the one&#8209;, three&#8209;, five-, and ten year periods&#160;ending March 31, 2026, all 17 Hennessy Funds&#160;posted positive total returns (except that the Hennessy Sustainable ETF does not have ten years of performance history).</p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">As always, we are committed to providing superior service to investors and employing a consistent and disciplined approach to investing based on a buy&#8209;and&#8209;hold philosophy that rejects the idea of market timing. Our goal is to provide products that investors can have confidence in, knowing their money is invested as promised and with their best interests in mind. Accordingly, we continually seek new and improved ways to support investors in the Hennessy Funds, including by providing market insights, sector highlights, and other resources to help them manage their fund investments with confidence. We operate a robust and leading&#8209;edge marketing automation and customer relationship management (CRM) system, with a database of over 100,000&#160;financial advisors, in addition to retail investors. We utilize this technology both to help retain assets and to drive new purchases into the Hennessy Funds. We employ a comprehensive marketing and sales program consisting of content, digital, social media, and traditional marketing initiatives and proactive meetings. In addition, our consistent annual public relations campaigns have resulted in the Hennessy brand name appearing on TV, radio, print, or online media on average once every four days.</p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">We provide service to nearly 170,000&#160;fund accounts nationwide, including accounts held by investors who employ financial advisors to assist them with investing as well as accounts held by retail investors who invest directly with us. We serve approximately 10,200&#160;financial advisors who utilize the Hennessy Funds on behalf of their clients, including roughly 200&#160;who purchased one of our Funds for the first time during the most recent quarter. Approximately 17%&#160;of such advisors own two or more Hennessy Funds, and approximately 700&#160;advisors hold a position of over $500,000. While numbers have declined in recent years, we continue to focus significant efforts on building and maintaining brand loyalty among our top tier of advisors.</p>
  <p style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; font-size: 10pt; margin: 0pt; text-align: left">&#160;</p>

The&#160;decrease in total assets was attributable to net outflows from the Hennessy Funds, partly offset by market appreciation.</p>

  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The principal asset on our balance sheet, the management contract&#160;asset, represents the capitalized costs incurred in connection with the purchase of the assets related to the management of investment funds. As of March 31, 2026, this asset had a net balance of $82.3&#160;million, compared to $82.6 million as of&#160;September 30, 2025.&#160;This decrease is related to a current period expense&#160;of previously capitalized costs as a result of the&#160;termination on January 1, 2026 of the definitive agreement signed with STF Management, LP in March 2025.</p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">On October 20, 2021, we completed a public offering of the 2026 Notes in the aggregate principal amount of $40.25&#160;million, which included the full exercise of the underwriters&#8217; overallotment option. The 2026 Notes mature on December 31, 2026, and may now be redeemed in whole or in part at any time or from time to time at our option. The 2026 Notes bear interest at 4.875% per annum, payable on the last day of each calendar quarter and at maturity, beginning December 31, 2021. The 2026 Notes are direct unsecured obligations, rank equally in right of payment with any of our future unsecured unsubordinated indebtedness, senior to any of our future indebtedness that expressly provides that it is subordinate to the 2026 Notes, effectively subordinate to all of our future secured indebtedness, and structurally subordinate&#160;to all future indebtedness and other obligations of any future subsidiaries of ours.</p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt;">The 2026 Notes are the principal liability on our balance sheet at $40.0 million, net of issuance costs. At December 31, 2025, the 2026 Notes were reclassified from long-term to current as the maturity date is within twelve months.&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; font-size: 10pt; margin: 0pt; text-align: left">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;"><b>Liquidity and Capital Resources </b></p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">We continually review our capital requirements to ensure that we have funding available to support our business model. Management anticipates that cash and other liquid assets on hand as of March 31, 2026, will be sufficient to meet our capital requirements for one year from the issuance date of this report, as well as our longer-term capital requirements for periods beyond one year from the issuance date of this report. To the extent that liquid resources and cash provided by operations are not adequate to meet long-term capital requirements, management plans to raise additional capital by either, or both, seeking bank financing or accessing the capital markets. There can be no assurance that we will be able to raise additional capital.</p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>

  <p style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; font-size: 10pt; margin: 0pt; text-align: left">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">As discussed above, on October&#160;20, 2021, we completed a public offering of our 2026&#160;Notes in the aggregate principal amount of $40.25&#160;million. The 2026 Notes mature on December&#160;31, 2026, and may now be redeemed in whole or in part at any time or from time to time at our option.&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">Our total assets under management as of March 31, 2026, was $3.9&#160;billion, a&#160;decrease&#160;of $0.3&#160;billion, or 7.8%, compared to March 31, 2025. The primary sources of our revenue, liquidity, and cash flow are our investment advisory fees and shareholder service fees, which are based on and generated by our average assets under management. Our average assets under management for the six months ended March 31, 2026, was&#160;$4.2&#160;billion, a decrease of $0.6 billion, or 12.9%, compared to the six months ended March 31, 2025.&#160;As of March 31, 2026, we had cash and cash equivalents of $73.0 million.</p>

  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The decrease in cash provided by operating activities of $3.0 million was primarily due to decreased net income&#160;and decreased accrued liabilities and accounts payable in the current period.</p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt;">The decrease in cash used in investing activities of $0.1&#160;million was due to decreased purchases of property and equipment&#160;in the current period.</p>
  <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">The increase in cash used in financing activities of $0.1&#160;million was due to the increased dollar amount of dividends paid as a result of an increased dividend rate and&#160;having more shares outstanding in the current&#160;period than in the prior comparable period.</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;&#160;</p>

  <p style="font-family: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; font-size: 10pt; margin: 0pt; text-align: left">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;"><i>Revenue </i>&#8211;<i> Investment Advisory Fees and Shareholder Service Fees</i></p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">Total revenue comprises investment advisory fees and shareholder service fees. Comparing the three months ended March 31, 2025, to the three months ended March 31, 2026, total revenue decreased&#160;by&#160;12.3%, from $9.3&#160;million to $8.1&#160;million, investment advisory fees&#160;decreased&#160;by&#160;12.6%, from $8.7&#160;million to $7.6&#160;million, and shareholder service fees&#160;decreased by 8.5%, from&#160;$0.60 million to&#160;$0.55 million.&#160;Comparing the six&#160;months ended March 31, 2025, to the six months ended March 31, 2026, total revenue&#160;decreased by&#160;13.3%, from $19.0&#160;million to $16.5&#160;million, investment advisory fees&#160;decreased by&#160;13.5%, from $17.7&#160;million to&#160;$15.3 million, and shareholder service fees&#160;decreased&#160;by 11.1%, from&#160;$1.3&#160;million to&#160;$1.1&#160;million.</p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">In each&#160;period, the decrease in investment advisory fees was due mainly to decreased average daily net assets of the Hennessy Funds, and the decrease in shareholder service fees was due to a&#160;decrease in the average daily net assets held in Investor Class shares of the Hennessy Mutual Funds. Assets held in Investor Class shares of the Hennessy Mutual Funds are subject to a shareholder service fee, whereas assets held in Institutional Class shares of the Hennessy Mutual Funds are not subject to a shareholder service fee.&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family:Times New Roman;font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;text-indent:36pt;">We collect investment advisory fees from each of the Hennessy Funds at differing annual rates. These annual rates range between 0.40% and 1.25% of average daily net assets. Average daily net assets of the Hennessy Funds for the three months ended March 31, 2026, was $4.2&#160;billion, which represents a&#160;decrease&#160;of $0.6&#160;billion, or&#160;12.2%,&#160;compared to the three months ended March 31, 2025, and average daily net assets of the Hennessy Funds for the six months ended March 31, 2026, was $4.2&#160;billion, which represents a&#160;decrease of $0.6&#160;billion, or&#160;12.9%,&#160;compared to the six months ended March 31, 2025.&#160;The Hennessy Fund with the largest average daily net assets for the three and six months ended March 31, 2026, was the Hennessy Cornerstone Mid Cap 30 Fund, with $1.2 billion for both periods.&#160;We collect an investment advisory fee from the Hennessy Cornerstone Mid Cap 30 Fund at an annual rate of 0.74% of average daily net assets. The Hennessy Fund with the second largest average daily assets for the three and six months ended March 31, 2026, was the Hennessy Focus&#160;Fund, with $0.5&#160;billion for both periods. We collect an investment advisory fee from the Hennessy Focus Fund at an annual rate of 0.90% of average daily net assets. However, we pay a sub&#8209;advisory fee at an annual rate of 0.29% to the fund&#8217;s sub-advisor, which reduces the net operating profit contribution of the fund to our financial operations.</p>
  <p style="font-family:'Times New Roman';font-size:10pt;font-variant:normal;margin:0pt;">&#160;</p>
  <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt; text-indent: 36pt;">Total assets under management as of March 31, 2026, was $3.9&#160;billion, a decrease of&#160;$0.3&#160;billion, or 7.8%, compared to March 31, 2025. The decrease in total assets was attributable to&#160;net outflows from the Hennessy Funds, partly offset by market appreciation.</p>
  <p style="font-size: 10pt; font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;; margin: 0pt;">&#160;</p>

     <td style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt; margin-left: 0pt; width: 36%; padding: 0px;"> <p style="font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt; font-variant: normal; margin: 0pt;">Hennessy Focus Fund</p> </td>
     <td style="width: 1%; font-family: &quot;Times New Roman&quot;, Times, serif; font-size: 10pt;">&#160;</td>
